Resilience moves from acceptable internal failures to fallback retrieval, queue backpressure routing, and finally circuit breakers with graceful degradation.\n\nCost thresholds get an auto-kill switch, and canary rollback triggers automatically if CSAT drops below 4.0 for an hour.\n\nNow mapping the AI layer itself, following the standard order: defenses, budgets, retrieval, model access, memory, orchestration, routing, feedback. Guardrails cover injection detection and PII redaction, validated against OWASP Top 10 coverage. Cost/latency budgets cap tokens and sessions, targeting sub-$0.05 queries and sub-2s retrieval. Human-in-the-loop gating flags low-confidence answers for review before Phase 3 exposure, requiring under 5% false positives.\n\nFor retrieval, hybrid search combining BM25 and dense vectors, 512-token sliding window chunks, re-ranked by recency — gated on 80%+ top-5 recall. Model access uses hosted Claude API with a separate embeddings endpoint, starting as a mock in Phase 0 before going live in Phase 1, with rate limits tested and a fallback provider lined up. Memory stays session-scoped only, no long-term chat history. Orchestration is a straightforward pipeline — validate, retrieve, prompt, call, format, log — proven end-to-end in Phase 0. Routing starts single-model, deferring complexity-based routing to Phase 4.\n\nFeedback involves structured logging of corrections, CSAT, retrieval quality, hallucinations, and cost/latency, gated on generating 100+ labeled examples weekly by Phase 2.\n\nNo exceptions to the standard build order. Deferred items: multi-language support (pull forward if non-English traffic exceeds 20%), advanced routing (pull forward if accuracy gaps exceed 5% between question types), CRM/ticketing integration (pull forward only if requested post-Phase 3, since it adds organizational complexity), and persistent chat history/threading (pull forward if CSAT data shows\n\n-gate or cited basis, satisfying the traceability requirement.\n\nGates all carry five fields, phase assignment, and a named consumer — V1 feeds Phase 1 hardening, V4 unlocks Phase 3 launch. Thresholds are tagged ASSUMPTION, REQUIREMENT, or UNKNOWN, keeping inferred SLOs separate from missing inputs like help-center volume. Cross-cutting threads run through every phase: security tightens from guardrails to compliance to audit, observability builds from logging to alerts, reproducibility covers versioning, and resilience escalates from fallback search to circuit breakers to auto-rollback. The AI layer itself layers defenses, budgets, then retrieval.\n\nmodel access, memory, orchestration, routing (deferred), feedback — each layer justified rather than assumed. No exceptions needed, standard ordering holds. Deferred items are named outright: multi-language, advanced routing, CRM integration, history, fine-tuning, each with a pull-forward trigger spelled out. keeping docs current without retraining is a win | V1 gate (80% recall benchmark on 50 real support Q&As) | If V1 recall <70%, pivot to hybrid (RAG + in-context fine-tune) or pure fine-tune; requires replanning and 2–4 week delay |\n| **Hosted LLM API vs. self-hosted** | **R3** (API rate limits, vendor lock-in, cost model, data residency all depend on this) | **Hosted API** (Anthropic Claude or OpenAI, whichever has negotiated pricing & SLA) | Cost <$0.05/query is achievable; pure vectors risks missing exact-match queries; BM25-only risks low recall on paraphrased questions | V3 gate (latency spike: <2s p99 for retrieval) + V1 recall check | If V3 latency >2s, descope to pure BM25; if V1 recall <75%, add lexical boost or expand vector index |\n| **Human-in-the-loop gating for deployment** | **R3** (deployment model, ops load, feature scope all depend on this) | **Yes:** flag uncertain answers (confidence <0.7) for support-team review before customer sees them | Hallucination rate is high enough to warrant review; human review is sustainable at expected load | V4 gate (human review SLA feasibility test: 1 reviewer handles 3h simulated peak) | If V4 SLA fails (>5 min queue backup), either auto-escalate answers >0.85 confidence, or defer human review to post-deployment feedback loop; blocks Phase 3 |\n\n### R2 Decisions (weeks of rework; validation required before dependents harden):\n\n- **Session-scoped memory (no persistent history):** Default; re-evaluate in Phase 2 if feedback shows users want multi-turn context (cheaper to add early than debug later). Validated by Phase 2 CSAT analysis.\n\n### R1 Decisions (one line each; cheap to reverse):\n\n- **Chat UI:** Simple textarea + button; upgrade to rich UI in Phase 2 if needed.\n- **Single model for all questions:** Don't route simple vs. complex; validate single-model accuracy in Phase 2 before adding routing.\n- **Multi-language:** English only in Phase 0–2; defer to Phase 4 if >20% traffic is non-English.\n\n### N/A (not applicable to this system):\n\n- **Consistency vs. availability:** Read-only retrieval system; no writes or consistency tradeoff.\n- **Monolith vs. microservices:** Single Lambda/container function handles all tiers; scale up later if needed.\n- **Sync vs. async:** Synchronous request–response (RAG must respond in <5s); async is deferred for long-running bulk analysis.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Walking Skeleton (Phase 0)\n\n**One real request, end-to-end:**\n\nUser asks: *\"How do I reset my password?\"*\n- Input is text (no validation or injection checks yet).\n- **Retrieval tier:** Query is embedded; top-5 docs retrieved from mock vector index (hardcoded for this phase).\n- **LLM tier:** Prompt is constructed: `You are a support assistant. Answer based on: [doc 1] [doc 2] ... User asks: How do I reset my password? Answer:` → sent to LLM API (live or mock).\n- **Output tier:** LLM response is formatted with source doc links and confidence score; logged with trace ID.\n- **Response:** JSON returned with answer text, sources, confidence, and cost estimate.\n\n**Tiers crossed:**\n1. API endpoint (REST or async Lambda)\n2. Input parsing (text only; no special chars yet)\n3. Embedding (query → vector)\n4. Retrieval (vector DB query + ranking)\n5. Prompt construction (few-shot template + context)\n6. LLM API call (streaming or non-streaming)\n7. Response formatting + logging\n8. Return to client\n\n**Deployment:**\n- **Runtime:** Serverless function (AWS Lambda / Google Cloud Function) or containerized task.\n- **Pipeline:** GitHub Actions / GitLab CI → build Docker image → push to staging ECR → run integration test → deploy to prod (internal endpoint only).\n- **Monitoring:** Structured JSON logging to stdout (CloudWatch / Stackdriver); no external dashboards yet; cost and latency metrics exported to a simple CSV file.\n- **Rollback:** Previous Docker image is one command; no data migrations (mock docs are ephemeral).\n\n**Who can reach it:**\n- **Internal only** (dev team + early-access support staff) behind a feature flag or on an internal network.\n- Mock help-center docs (sanitized, no real customer data).\n- No rate limiting; run cost cap but accept overages for testing.\n\n**Hardening level (deliberately NOT hardened):**\n- No prompt injection filter (for speed; add in Phase 1).\n- No token counter (run at full capacity; add hard cap in Phase 1).\n- No human review queue (answers go straight out; Phase 2 adds review).\n- No compliance checks (will add in Phase 2).\n- **Hard limits only:** Max 500 output tokens per response; max 3 retries on rate limit; session budget $1 (absolute stop).\n\n**Exit check V0:**\n1. ✅ One real question is routed through every tier, LLM responds, answer is logged with trace ID.\n2. ✅ Deploy pipeline executes: code → image → staging test → prod deploy without error.\n3. ✅ Rollback to prior image version succeeds; no data loss.\n4. ✅ Injected failure test: retrieval times out → function catches error, logs it, returns fallback response (\"contact support\") → alarm fires in CloudWatch.\n5. ✅ Baseline metrics recorded: latency (cold start, warm start), cost per query (embedding + LLM), throughput (QPS).\n6. ✅ One full internal user session logged end-to-end with trace visible in CloudWatch.\n\n**Evidence kept:** CloudWatch logs (30-day retention), CI/CD pipeline run history, Docker image digest tagged in ECR.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Phases\n\n### Phase 0 (days 1–3): Walking Skeleton\n*Unlocks:* Production-ready code path; confirms LLM provider is reachable; deployment pipeline works.\n*Depends on:* LLM provider contract signed; help-center mock data ready; dev environment access.\n*Tasks:*\n- Scaffold Lambda function: input parsing → embedding call → retrieval stub (hardcoded mock results) → LLM call → response formatting.\n- Integrate with LLM API (Anthropic Claude or OpenAI; assume Anthropic for this plan).\n- Set up CI/CD pipeline: GitHub → Actions → ECR push → Lambda deploy.\n- Add structured JSON logging to stdout (trace ID, latency, cost estimate).\n- Local integration test: one question end-to-end.\n- Deploy to staging; run test; promote to internal prod (no customer traffic).\n\n*Rollback:* Prior Docker image redeploy; no state to recover.\n\n*Exit check V0:* One question succeeds end-to-end; deploy and rollback both succeed; failure injection triggers alert; baseline latency/cost recorded.\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Hard token limit (500 out) + session cost cap ($1) enforced; mock docs only (no real customer data).\n- **Observability:** Structured JSON logs with trace ID on every request; latency + cost metrics logged; no dashboard yet (manual CSV export).\n- **Reproducibility:** Code in Git (main branch); requirements.txt pinned; Docker image tagged with Git commit SHA.\n- **Resilience:** Function catches LLM API timeouts and returns fallback (\"contact support\"); no retries yet (add in Phase 1).\n\n---\n\n### Phase 1 (days 4–10): Foundation — Retrieval Quality + Guardrails\n*Unlocks:* Validated retrieval accuracy and cost model; prompt injection defenses in place; hardened retrieval indexing.\n*Depends on:* V0 passed; real help-center docs available (schema known); LLM provider contract and rate limits confirmed.\n*Tasks:*\n- **Retrieval indexing:**\n  - Ingest real help-center docs (Markdown/HTML) → parse → chunk (512-token sliding windows, 50% overlap).\n  - Generate embeddings (Cohere or OpenAI; assume Anthropic's models for compatibility) → load into vector DB (Pinecone, Weaviate, or local FAISS).\n  - Build BM25 index (Elasticsearch or Whoosh) for lexical fallback.\n  - Implement hybrid retrieval: vector search + BM25, combine rankings by normalized score.\n  - Add re-ranking: boost recency, doc quality score (manual curation), relevance confidence.\n- **Prompt injection & guardrail defense:**\n  - Input filter: regex patterns for common jailbreak attempts; length limit (e.g., <500 chars per question).\n  - Output filter: redact email/phone patterns; flag contradictions (e.g., LLM says \"yes\" but top doc says \"no\").\n  - Token counter: track every embedding and LLM call; enforce per-session budget ($1 hard limit).\n- **Observability hardening:**\n  - Query-level logging: question, top-5 retrieved doc IDs, LLM prompt (first 200 chars), tokens (input/output), cost, latency, user ID (anonymized).\n  - Create simple metrics dashboard: latency histogram, cost/query distribution, retrieval recall estimate.\n  - Alert: cost spike alert if >2x baseline per minute.\n- **Validation gates:**\n  - Run V1 (retrieval accuracy): 50 representative Q&As from past support tickets; hand-grade top-5 recall. Target ≥80%.\n  - Run V2 (cost baseline): 100 live queries; track total spend and p50/p95/p99. Target <$0.05/query.\n  - Run V3 (latency): 1000 retrieval-only queries; measure latency distribution. Target p99 <2s retrieval (excl. LLM).\n\n*Rollback:* Revert code to Phase 0; retrieval index can be deleted (re-indexed from Phase 0 mock data if needed); no breaking changes to API.\n\n*Exit checks:* V1 ≥80% recall, V2 <$0.05/query, V3 p99 <2s retrieval. All three gates pass before Phase 2 hardening begins.\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Prompt injection input/output filters active; cost counter enforced; rate limiting on LLM API calls (no unlimited retries).\n- **Observability:** Full request/response logging (question, retrieval results, LLM prompt/response, cost, latency); metrics dashboard created; alerts configured.\n- **Reproducibility:** Help-center doc snapshot versioned (commit hash in log); embedding model version tagged; retrieval index version labeled.\n- **Resilience:** Fallback to BM25 if vector DB times out; exponential backoff on LLM rate limits; graceful error message to user if retrievals fail (don't hang).\n\n---\n\n### Phase 2 (days 11–17): Safety Hardening + Human Review\n*Unlocks:* Human review loop validated and operational; compliance review passed; ready for closed-beta internal deployment.\n*Depends on:* Phase 1 exit checks passed (V1, V2, V3 all green); real support team assigned (1 reviewer minimum).\n*Tasks:*\n- **Human review queue:**\n  - Build simple UI: support team sees flagged answers (confidence <0.7), marks as \"correct,\" \"needs edit,\" or \"hallucination.\"\n  - Timestamp and log each review; track SLA (target: 95% reviewed within 30 min).\n  - Implement queue backoff: if reviews back up >5 min, chatbot temporarily stops accepting new questions and queues them.\n- **Compliance review:**\n  - Data classification audit: does help-center contain PII, regulated data (HIPAA, GDPR, PCI-DSS)?\n  - If yes: mask PII before retrieval context goes to LLM (e.g., redact customer email patterns from examples).\n  - Sign-off from legal/privacy: data residency, retention, audit trail, export/deletion procedures.\n- **CSAT & quality feedback:**\n  - Add simple 1–5 star rating to each chatbot answer.\n  - Log CSAT per session; correlate with confidence score, human review outcome.\n  - Collect correction feedback from support team (what did the chatbot get wrong?).\n- **A/B framework (optional):**\n  - For internal users only: 50% see chatbot, 50% see support queue. Track resolution rate, time-to-answer.\n- **Validation gates:**\n  - Run V4 (human review capacity): 1 reviewer processes 3 hours of peak simulated traffic (100 messages). Measure: queue depth, review latency. Target: 95% <30 min.\n  - Compliance review checklist: legal sign-off on PII handling, data residency, audit logging. Target: \"pass\" from privacy team.\n\n*Rollback:* Revert code to Phase 1; human review queue logs are kept for audit; no customer data exposed yet (internal team only).\n\n*Exit checks:* V4 (human review SLA met), compliance review passed, CSAT baseline captured (internal team), support team trained on queue.\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Compliance sign-off required; PII masking in retrieval context; human review logs stored separately (sensitive).\n- **Observability:** Human review queue dashboarded (queue depth, review latency, correction frequency); CSAT trend tracked; correction signals logged separately for future model tuning.\n- **Reproducibility:** Help-center snapshot locked per session (so corrections reference the exact docs); human review feedback versioned (commit per week).\n- **Resilience:** If review queue backs up >5 min, chatbot enters \"queue-only mode\" (accepts questions but routes to support fallback); manual override to close queue if needed.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 3 (days 18–24): Canary Exposure + Monitored Rollout\n*Unlocks:* Public chatbot for all customers; 100% traffic once canary SLO met.\n*Depends on:* Phase 2 exit checks passed (V4, compliance review, internal CSAT); runbook + on-call setup ready; support team trained on escalation.\n*Tasks:*\n- **Canary deployment (5% traffic):**\n  - Feature flag: route 5% of support traffic to chatbot, 95% to traditional support queue (baseline).\n  - Monitor for 1 week: latency, error rate, CSAT, escalation rate.\n  - Automatic circuit breaker: if p99 latency >5s OR error rate >1% OR CSAT <4.0/5 for >1 hour, rollback feature flag (revert to 100% traditional support).\n- **Runbook & on-call:**\n  - Escalation path: support team sees alert → checks dashboard → decides (override queue back open, or extend canary).\n  - Manual override: support lead can toggle feature flag and adjust traffic split without deploy.\n  - Rollback procedure: feature flag off; chatbot stops accepting new questions; in-flight questions time out gracefully.\n- **Real-time observability:**\n  - Support team dashboard: CSAT trend, top unanswered questions, error log, chatbot load vs. capacity.\n  - Alerts: CSAT drop, error spike, latency spike, cost per query spike, queue backup.\n- **Canary metrics validation:**\n  - Run V5 gate: after 1 week at 5%, check: p99 latency <5s, uptime >99.5%, CSAT >4.0/5, error rate <1%. Support lead sign-off required.\n  - If V5 passes: promote to 100% (feature flag opens to all traffic).\n  - If V5 fails: extend canary (1 more week) or rollback to Phase 2 (internal only).\n\n*Rollback:* Feature flag off → all traffic to traditional support; no data loss (all interactions logged in chatbot queue for post-mortem).\n\n*Point of no return:* Once chatbot handles >50% of live traffic and CSAT trend is negative for >1 hour, circuit breaker auto-triggers rollback (no manual approval needed).\n\n*Exit checks:* V5 gate passed; runbook tested once; support team alerts ACKed and responded to; 1 week of 5% canary stable.\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Rate limiting per user (max 10 questions/min); IP-based blocking for injection-like patterns; all interactions audit-logged (compliance required).\n- **Observability:** Real-time dashboards (support team), automated alerting (Slack/email to on-call), CSAT + error + latency tracked per hour, canary metrics published.\n- **Reproducibility:** Chatbot response version + LLM model version + help-center snapshot version logged per interaction (for debugging); corrections logged separately.\n- **Resilience:** Circuit breaker on error spike (auto-rollback); graceful \"contact support\" fallback if LLM unavailable; cost cutoff switch ($X/hour hard limit, stops new queries).\n\n---\n\n### Phase 4 (day 25+): Optimization & Feedback Loop\n*Unlocks:* Fine-tuning candidates identified; advanced routing (deferred); multi-language support (deferred); performance improvements.\n*Depends on:* Phase 3 canary + full launch stable for 2+ weeks; feedback data collected.\n*Tasks:*\n- **Analyze human corrections & CSAT:**\n  - Identify systematic hallucinations (e.g., \"reset password\" always says \"click Settings\" but some docs say \"Account Preferences\").\n  - Identify retrieval gaps (questions where top-5 docs don't contain answer).\n  - Identify high-value reductions (if 20 questions converge on same doc, consider adding FAQ link).\n- **Iterate on retrieval:**\n  - Re-chunk help docs if needed (smaller or larger chunks?).\n  - Adjust embedding model or ranking weights based on CSAT correlation.\n  - Add new docs or update stale docs based on correction feedback.\n- **Optional fine-tuning (data-driven):**\n  - If >100 high-confidence (CSAT=5, no hallucination) interactions are collected, consider fine-tuning LLM on these (Phase 4+).\n  - Test fine-tune on isolated cohort before production rollout.\n- **Prepare deferred work (not implemented yet):**\n  - Multi-language support: collect non-English queries; plan embedding + docs translation (Phase 5).\n  - Advanced routing: analyze if simple vs. complex questions diverge in accuracy; plan route model (Phase 5).\n  - CRM integration: support team requests; plan ticket linkage and data sync (Phase 5).\n\n*Rollback:* N/A (feedback loop; no breaking changes).\n\n*Exit checks:* Correction feedback analyzed; top 5 hallucination patterns identified and logged; retrieval re-ranking evaluated (before/after CSAT impact).\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Audit corrections for PII leaks; redact future retrieval contexts if patterns found; tighten output filters.\n- **Observability:** Feedback loop dashboard (correction types, hallucination frequency, retrieval miss rate); model performance drift detection (CSAT trend).\n- **Reproducibility:** Fine-tuning datasets versioned (if used); model checkpoint + version tagged per week; before/after metrics compared.\n- **Resilience:** Monitor for feedback loop collapse (corrections amplifying errors); keep human review threshold high until retraining validates.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Validation Gates\n\n| Gate ID | Hypothesis | Method | Acceptance threshold | Evidence | Unlocks | Phase |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **V0** | Real request reaches LLM end-to-end and returns answer; pipeline deploys and rolls back cleanly | One internal user asks \"How do I reset my password?\"; answer logged with trace ID; deploy to prod via CI/CD; rollback to prior image | Answer returned within 30s with trace visible; deploy <2 min; rollback <1 min; error injection triggers alert; baseline latency (cold start, warm start) recorded | CloudWatch JSON log with full request/response/trace ID; ECR image tags; CI/CD run history showing deploy + rollback | Phase 1 retrieval + Phase 1 guardrails work begins | Phase 0 (days 1–3) |\n| **V1** | Retrieval returns relevant docs in top-5 with ≥80% recall | 50 representative Q&As from past support tickets; run each through live retrieval; hand-grade whether correct answer appears in top-5 | ≥80% of questions have relevant doc in top-5; median rank of correct answer ≤2 | CSV: question_id, top_5_doc_ids, human_graded_recall (Y/N), rank_of_correct_doc | Phase 1 hardening complete; V1 failure triggers retrieval re-plan (expand index, adjust ranking, or pivot strategy) | Phase 1 (starts day 4, required before Phase 2) |\n| **V2** | Cost per query is economically viable | 100 representative queries run through live LLM API + embedding endpoint; record token counts and API charges | <$0.05/query median; <$0.10/query p95 (includes embedding + LLM call) | JSON metrics: query_id, embedding_cost, llm_input_tokens, llm_output_tokens, llm_cost, total_cost; API provider receipts | Phase 1 hardening; cost model validated; if >$0.10 p95, consider cheaper model or retrieval optimization | Phase 1 (starts day 4, required before Phase 2) |\n| **V3** | Retrieval latency meets SLO (p99 <2s) | 1000 retrieval-only queries (embeddings + vector DB + ranking, excl. LLM); measure latency | p50 <500ms; p95 <1s; p99 <2s | Latency histogram from benchmarking harness; time-series plot of p50/p95/p99 | Phase 1 hardening; if failed, evaluate BM25 fallback or index tuning; signals readiness for LLM integration | Phase 1 (starts day 4, required before Phase 2) |\n| **V4** | Human review team can sustain 95% of reviews within 30-min SLO | Simulate 3 hours of peak traffic (100 msgs/hour); 1 reviewer processes queue; measure time-to-review and queue depth | 95% of answers reviewed within 30 min; reviewer does not exceed 3 msgs/min (sustainable pace); queue never exceeds 10 items | Timestamped review queue log; time-to-review histogram; reviewer workload estimate | Phase 2 hardening; determines if human-in-the-loop gating is operationally viable; V4 failure blocks Phase 3 or triggers queue automation (auto-escalate high-confidence answers) | Phase 2 (starts day 11, required before Phase 3) |\n| **V5** | System meets SLO and customer experience bar in 5% canary | Route 5% of live traffic to chatbot for 1 week; measure latency, uptime, CSAT, error rate | p99 latency <5s; uptime >99.5%; CSAT >4.0/5 (from 1–5 rating); error rate <1% | Canary metrics dashboard exported as CSV; CSAT survey results; error logs from CloudWatch | Phase 3 → full 100% launch; auto-rollback if V5 metric breaches for >1 hour | Phase 3 (starts day 18, required before 100% traffic) |\n| **Compliance Review** | Help-center data can be legally exposed to LLM inference without violating privacy/security obligations | Legal + Privacy team audits help-center docs for PII, regulated data (HIPAA, GDPR, PCI-DSS); reviews LLM vendor data handling; approves retrieval context masking rules | Sign-off from Privacy Lead on: (1) PII masking rules (email/phone redacted), (2) data residency acceptable, (3) audit logging sufficient | Email approval from Privacy team; documented PII masking rules; audit log format | Phase 2 exit; Phase 3 canary launch; V0 if fails (no exposure until resolved) | Phase 2 (starts day 11, required before Phase 3) |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Cross-cutting Concerns\n\n| Concern | Phase 0 | Phase 1 | Phase 2 | Phase 3 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **Security** | Hard token limit (500 out); session cost cap ($1); mock docs only (no real data) | Prompt injection input filter (jailbreak detection); output filter (PII redaction, contradiction flagging); token counter enforced; rate limiting on LLM API | Compliance clearance gate (Privacy team sign-off on PII handling); role-based access control for human review queue (support team only); audit log separate from application logs | Rate limiting per user (10 q/min); IP-based blocking for injection patterns; real-time audit logging; circuit breaker on injection spike; all interactions audit-logged with user context |\n| **Observability** | Structured JSON logging to stdout (trace ID, latency, cost); latency + cost metrics exported to CSV at phase end; no dashboard yet | Query-level logging (question, top-5 doc IDs, LLM prompt first 200 chars, tokens, cost, latency, user anonymized); metrics dashboard created (latency histogram, cost distribution, retrieval recall); alerts on cost spike (>2x baseline) | Human review queue metrics dashboarded (queue depth, review latency p50/p95, correction frequency); CSAT trend tracked per day; correction signals logged separately | Real-time dashboards for support team (CSAT trend, top unanswered questions, error log, chatbot QPS vs. capacity); automated alerts to Slack/email on SLO breach (latency >5s, CSAT <4.0, error >1%); canary metrics published hourly |\n| **Reproducibility** | Code in Git (main branch); requirements.txt pinned (all deps versioned); Docker image tagged with Git SHA; mock data set versioned | Help-center doc snapshot tagged (commit hash logged); embedding model version logged; retrieval index version labeled; V1/V2/V3 benchmark data saved | Same as Phase 1; human review feedback versioned (commit per week); PII masking rule version tracked | Same as Phase 2; model version + LLM provider + help-center version logged per interaction (for debugging); canary traffic split + feature flag version logged |\n| **Resilience** | N/A (internal only; graceful failure acceptable; no SLO) | Fallback to BM25 if vector DB times out; exponential backoff on LLM rate limits; timeout guard (5s) on all external calls; graceful error response (\"Contact support\") if retrieval fails | If human review queue backs up >5 min, chatbot enters read-only mode (queues questions, routes to support fallback); manual override available to support lead to close queue | Circuit breaker: if p99 latency >5s OR error >1% OR CSAT <4.0 for >1 hour → auto-rollback feature flag (stop 100% launch, revert to canary or Phase 2); cost cutoff switch ($X/hour hard limit stops new queries); graceful \"Contact support\" fallback if LLM unavailable |\n\n---\n\n## 8. AI Layer\n\nPer principle 7: **defenses → budgets → retrieval → model access → memory → orchestration → routing → feedback**.\n\n| Sublayer | What it is here | Phase built | Exit check / V-ID |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **Guardrails / Prompt injection defense** | Input filter: regex patterns for jailbreak (SQL, code execution, role-switching attempts); output filter: redact email/phone; flag contradictions vs. top docs | Phase 1 | V1 recall check validates no malicious prompts escape to LLM; output filter tested on 20 injection payloads (must redact/flag all) |\n| **Cost + latency budget** | Hard token cap: max 500 output tokens per response (auto-truncate); per-session cap: max $1 (stop all queries if exceeded); retrieval + LLM latency SLO: p99 <5s | Phase 0 (skeleton hard limits); Phase 1 (soft tracking) | V2 gate: <$0.05/query median validates cost model sustainable; V3 gate: p99 <2s retrieval latency validates feasibility |\n| **Human-in-the-loop gating** | Flag answers with confidence <0.7 for support-team review queue before customer sees them; CSAT + correction feedback loop | Phase 2 | V4 gate: human review SLA (95% <30 min) validates operationally feasible; Phase 3 canary: rollback if >5% of answers require correction |\n| **Retrieval** | Hybrid search: BM25 (lexical) + dense vector (semantic); chunk docs 512 tokens, 50% overlap; re-rank by recency + doc quality score + relevance confidence | Phase 1 | V1 gate: 80%+ recall in top-5 validates retrieval strategy; if <70%, replan to pure BM25 or expand index |\n| **Model access** | Hosted LLM API (assume Anthropic Claude for this plan; fallback to OpenAI if contract not available); embeddings from same vendor or Cohere | Phase 0 (mock); Phase 1 (live integration) | LLM provider contract signed (organizational check); rate limits confirmed; fallback provider identified by Phase 1; live API test in V0 |\n| **Memory** | Session-scoped conversation history (no persistent user profile); question-answer pairs only (not full chat); ephemeral by design | Phase 2 (scope confirmed) | Deferred: multi-turn context; re-evaluate in Phase 3 if CSAT feedback warrants (users want history) |\n| **Orchestration** | Simple linear pipeline: validate input → embed query → retrieve docs → construct prompt → call LLM → format output → log → return | Phase 0 | V0 gate: one trace through all steps succeeds |\n| **Routing** | Single LLM model for all question types (no routing logic); deferred: separate models for simple vs. complex questions | Phase 0–2 | Single-model accuracy baseline established in Phase 2 A/B test; re-evaluate in Phase 4 if accuracy diverges by question type |\n| **Feedback** | Structured logging of: human review corrections, CSAT ratings, retrieval quality (correct doc in top-5?), hallucinations flagged, cost/latency skew | Phase 2 | V4 gate: >100 labeled correction examples collected per week by Phase 2 validates feedback loop density; Phase 4 analysis determines if fine-tuning warranted |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Methodology Exceptions\n\n**None.** Standard dependency-first order applies. Walking skeleton (V0), defenses (guardrails, budgets), retrieval validation (V1–V3), human-in-the-loop (V4), compliance review, canary SLO (V5), and feedback loop proceed in sequence. No time-boxed spike or exceptional constraints justify reordering.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Deliberately Deferred\n\n| Feature | Condition to pull forward | Why deferred now |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Multi-language support** | If >20% of support traffic is non-English (measured in Phase 3) | English-only in Phase 0–2 simplifies Phase 0 skeleton; i18n of help docs + embeddings is R2 refactor that can wait until demand signals it |\n| **Advanced routing (question-type-specific models)** | If accuracy diverges >5% between \"technical\" vs. \"billing\" questions (measured in Phase 2 feedback) | Single model is simpler for Phase 0; routing adds orchestration complexity; wait for data to justify it |\n| **CRM / ticketing integration** | If support team requests it post-Phase 3 (e.g., \"link chat answers to tickets\") | Read-only help-center wrapper in Phase 0–3 is sufficient to validate core RAG quality; integration is organizational/process work, not critical path to launch |\n| **Persistent chat history & multi-turn context** | If Phase 3 CSAT analysis shows users want context retention (e.g., \"remember my previous question\") | Session-scoped conversation is simpler for Phase 0–2; adding history requires user model, retention policies (GDPR?), and persistence layer; wait for feedback |\n| **Fine-tuning on corrected interactions** | If Phase 3 feedback loop yields >500 high-confidence corrected interactions AND systematic hallucination pattern emerges | Fine-tuning is R3 (model retraining, reproducibility, version control); Phase 4 is earliest if data density justifies it; meanwhile, retrieval re-ranking and doc updates are faster wins |\n| **Self-hosted LLM model** | If Phase 1 V2 gate shows cost >$0.10/query OR compliance review mandates on-prem data residency | Hosted API (Anthropic/OpenAI) is faster to market; self-hosting adds ops burden (GPU provisioning, monitoring, failover); defer unless forced by economics or compliance |\n\n---\n\n## Self-Check\n\n✅ **Phase 0 is a production walking skeleton:** REST API endpoint, real question routed through every tier (embedding, retrieval, LLM), response logged with trace ID, deployed via CI/CD pipeline, rollback tested. Internal-only (feature flag or network isolation). Runs with unvalidated defaults (no guardrails, no human review, mock docs).\n\n✅ **Tasks follow dependency order:** V0 skeleton → Phase 1 (retrieval quality V1 + cost V2 + latency V3 + guardrails) → Phase 2 (human review SLA V4 + compliance review) → Phase 3 (canary SLO V5 + runbook) → Phase 4 (feedback loop + optimization).\n\n✅ **Rework runs widest first, exposure smallest first:** Retrieval architecture (V1 recall) and cost model (V2) validated before human review queue added (Phase 1 before Phase 2). Dependency Map\n\n**Binding dependencies** (the order they block work):\n\n| Dependency | Kind(s) | Blocks | Impact / What it unlocks |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **LLM provider availability** | Structural, Organizational | Phase 0 skeleton build | Cannot test end-to-end without LLM; assume Anthropic contract in place |\n| **Help-center docs schema known** | Structural, Validation | Phase 1 retrieval | Determines chunking, embedding strategy, retrieval ranking |\n| **Prompt injection defense design** | Risk/Security, Structural | Phase 1 hardening | Must be in place before human review; blocks Phase 2 if missing |\n| **Cost per query validated <$0.05** | Economic, Validation | Phase 1 hardening | If >$0.05, model selection or retrieval strategy must change; blocks Phase 2 without confirmation |\n| **Retrieval accuracy ≥80% confirmed** | Validation | Phase 1 hardening → Phase 2 | If <70%, RAG strategy pivots or fails; V1 gate blocks all downstream work until resolved |\n| **Human review capacity SLA (95% <30 min)** | Organizational, Validation | Phase 2 hardening | Determines whether human-in-the-loop gating is operationally viable; V4 blocks Phase 3 |\n| **Compliance review & PII audit passed** | Risk/Security, Organizational | Phase 3 exposure | Legal sign-off on data classification, retention, and export controls; no customer traffic until cleared |\n| **Canary SLO met (99.5% uptime, CSAT >4.0/5)** | Validation | Phase 3 → full 100% launch | Automatic rollback on fail; V5 gate required before wide exposure |\n\n**No dependencies of these kinds in this system:**\n- Brownfield migration or cutover (greenfield).\n- Multi-tenant or cross-org isolation (single customer support cohort initially).\n- Complex orchestration (simple pipeline).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Tradeoff Gates\n\n### Major R3 Decisions (hard to reverse; data model, cost structure, or customer-visible behavior locked in by skeleton):\n\n| Decision | Reversibility | Default | Assumption | Validated by | Flip condition |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **RAG vs. fine-tune** | **R3** (retrieval model, indexing schema, cost structure all depend on this) | **RAG** (retrieval-augmented generation over live help docs) | Retrieval quality will reach ≥80% recall in top-5; keeping docs current without retraining is a win | V1 gate (80% recall benchmark on 50 real support Q&As) | If V1 recall <70%, pivot to hybrid (RAG + in-context fine-tune) or pure fine-tune; requires replanning and 2–4 week delay |\n| **Hosted LLM API vs. self-hosted** | **R3** (API rate limits, vendor lock-in, cost model, data residency all depend on this) | **Hosted API** (Anthropic Claude or OpenAI, whichever has negotiated pricing & SLA) | Cost <$0.05/query is achievable; latency SLA met without on-prem ops; data residency acceptable to compliance | V2 gate (cost benchmark on 100 queries at live endpoint) + compliance review | If V2 cost >$0.10/query or data residency fails compliance review, move to self-hosted (adds 3–4 weeks ops setup) |\n| **Retrieval: hybrid (BM25 + vectors) vs. vectors-only** | **R2** (retrieval tier can change without rebuild, but ranking changes require re-indexing) | **Hybrid** (BM25 + dense vectors, re-ranked by recency & doc quality score) | Hybrid balances recall + speed; pure vectors risks missing exact-match queries; BM25-only risks low recall on paraphrased questions | V3 gate (latency spike: <2s p99 for retrieval) + V1 recall check | If V3 latency >2s, descope to pure BM25; if V1 recall <75%, add lexical boost or expand vector index |\n| **Human-in-the-loop gating for deployment** | **R3** (deployment model, ops load, feature scope all depend on this) | **Yes:** flag uncertain answers (confidence <0.7) for support-team review before customer sees them | Hallucination rate is high enough to warrant review; human review is sustainable at expected load | V4 gate (human review SLA feasibility test: 1 reviewer handles 3h simulated peak) | If V4 SLA fails (>5 min queue backup), either auto-escalate answers >0.85 confidence, or defer human review to post-deployment feedback loop; blocks Phase 3 |\n\n### R2 Decisions (weeks of rework; validation required before dependents harden):\n\n- **Session-scoped memory (no persistent history):** Default; re-evaluate in Phase 2 if feedback shows users want multi-turn context (cheaper to add early than debug later). Validated by Phase 2 CSAT analysis.\n\n### R1 Decisions (one line each; cheap to reverse):\n\n- **Chat UI:** Simple textarea + button; upgrade to rich UI in Phase 2 if needed.\n- **Single model for all questions:** Don't route simple vs. complex; validate single-model accuracy in Phase 2 before adding routing.\n- **Multi-language:** English only in Phase 0–2; defer to Phase 4 if >20% traffic is non-English.\n\n### N/A (not applicable to this system):\n\n- **Consistency vs. availability:** Read-only retrieval system; no writes or consistency tradeoff.\n- **Monolith vs. microservices:** Single Lambda/container function handles all tiers; scale up later if needed.\n- **Sync vs. async:** Synchronous request–response (RAG must respond in <5s); async is deferred for long-running bulk analysis.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Walking Skeleton (Phase 0)\n\n**One real request, end-to-end:**\n\nUser asks: *\"How do I reset my password?\"*\n- Input is text (no validation or injection checks yet).\n- **Retrieval tier:** Query is embedded; top-5 docs retrieved from mock vector index (hardcoded for this phase).\n- **LLM tier:** Prompt is constructed: `You are a support assistant. Answer based on: [doc 1] [doc 2] ... User asks: How do I reset my password? Answer:` → sent to LLM API (live or mock).\n- **Output tier:** LLM response is formatted with source doc links and confidence score; logged with trace ID.\n- **Response:** JSON returned with answer text, sources, confidence, and cost estimate.\n\n**Tiers crossed:**\n1. API endpoint (REST or async Lambda)\n2. Input parsing (text only; no special chars yet)\n3. Embedding (query → vector)\n4. Retrieval (vector DB query + ranking)\n5. Prompt construction (few-shot template + context)\n6. LLM API call (streaming or non-streaming)\n7. Response formatting + logging\n8. Return to client\n\n**Deployment:**\n- **Runtime:** Serverless function (AWS Lambda / Google Cloud Function) or containerized task.\n- **Pipeline:** GitHub Actions / GitLab CI → build Docker image → push to staging ECR → run integration test → deploy to prod (internal endpoint only).\n- **Monitoring:** Structured JSON logging to stdout (CloudWatch / Stackdriver); no external dashboards yet; cost and latency metrics exported to a simple CSV file.\n- **Rollback:** Previous Docker image is one command; no data migrations (mock docs are ephemeral).\n\n**Who can reach it:**\n- **Internal only** (dev team + early-access support staff) behind a feature flag or on an internal network.\n- Mock help-center docs (sanitized, no real customer data).\n- No rate limiting; run cost cap but accept overages for testing.\n\n**Hardening level (deliberately NOT hardened):**\n- No prompt injection filter (for speed; add in Phase 1).\n- No token counter (run at full capacity; add hard cap in Phase 1).\n- No human review queue (answers go straight out; Phase 2 adds review).\n- No compliance checks (will add in Phase 2).\n- **Hard limits only:** Max 500 output tokens per response; max 3 retries on rate limit; session budget $1 (absolute stop).\n\n**Exit check V0:**\n1. ✅ One real question is routed through every tier, LLM responds, answer is logged with trace ID.\n2. ✅ Deploy pipeline executes: code → image → staging test → prod deploy without error.\n3. ✅ Rollback to prior image version succeeds; no data loss.\n4. ✅ Injected failure test: retrieval times out → function catches error, logs it, returns fallback response (\"contact support\") → alarm fires in CloudWatch.\n5. ✅ Baseline metrics recorded: latency (cold start, warm start), cost per query (embedding + LLM), throughput (QPS).\n6. ✅ One full internal user session logged end-to-end with trace visible in CloudWatch.\n\n**Evidence kept:** CloudWatch logs (30-day retention), CI/CD pipeline run history, Docker image digest tagged in ECR.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Phases\n\n### Phase 0 (days 1–3): Walking Skeleton\n*Unlocks:* Production-ready code path; confirms LLM provider is reachable; deployment pipeline works.\n*Depends on:* LLM provider contract signed; help-center mock data ready; dev environment access.\n*Tasks:*\n- Scaffold Lambda function: input parsing → embedding call → retrieval stub (hardcoded mock results) → LLM call → response formatting.\n- Integrate with LLM API (Anthropic Claude or OpenAI; assume Anthropic for this plan).\n- Set up CI/CD pipeline: GitHub → Actions → ECR push → Lambda deploy.\n- Add structured JSON logging to stdout (trace ID, latency, cost estimate).\n- Local integration test: one question end-to-end.\n- Deploy to staging; run test; promote to internal prod (no customer traffic).\n\n*Rollback:* Prior Docker image redeploy; no state to recover.\n\n*Exit check V0:* One question succeeds end-to-end; deploy and rollback both succeed; failure injection triggers alert; baseline latency/cost recorded.\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Hard token limit (500 out) + session cost cap ($1) enforced; mock docs only (no real customer data).\n- **Observability:** Structured JSON logs with trace ID on every request; latency + cost metrics logged; no dashboard yet (manual CSV export).\n- **Reproducibility:** Code in Git (main branch); requirements.txt pinned; Docker image tagged with Git commit SHA.\n- **Resilience:** Function catches LLM API timeouts and returns fallback (\"contact support\"); no retries yet (add in Phase 1).\n\n---\n\n### Phase 1 (days 4–10): Foundation — Retrieval Quality + Guardrails\n*Unlocks:* Validated retrieval accuracy and cost model; prompt injection defenses in place; hardened retrieval indexing.\n*Depends on:* V0 passed; real help-center docs available (schema known); LLM provider contract and rate limits confirmed.\n*Tasks:*\n- **Retrieval indexing:**\n  - Ingest real help-center docs (Markdown/HTML) → parse → chunk (512-token sliding windows, 50% overlap).\n  - Generate embeddings (Cohere or OpenAI; assume Anthropic's models for compatibility) → load into vector DB (Pinecone, Weaviate, or local FAISS).\n  - Build BM25 index (Elasticsearch or Whoosh) for lexical fallback.\n  - Implement hybrid retrieval: vector search + BM25, combine rankings by normalized score.\n  - Add re-ranking: boost recency, doc quality score (manual curation), relevance confidence.\n- **Prompt injection & guardrail defense:**\n  - Input filter: regex patterns for common jailbreak attempts; length limit (e.g., <500 chars per question).\n  - Output filter: redact email/phone patterns; flag contradictions (e.g., LLM says \"yes\" but top doc says \"no\").\n  - Token counter: track every embedding and LLM call; enforce per-session budget ($1 hard limit).\n- **Observability hardening:**\n  - Query-level logging: question, top-5 retrieved doc IDs, LLM prompt (first 200 chars), tokens (input/output), cost, latency, user ID (anonymized).\n  - Create simple metrics dashboard: latency histogram, cost/query distribution, retrieval recall estimate.\n  - Alert: cost spike alert if >2x baseline per minute.\n- **Validation gates:**\n  - Run V1 (retrieval accuracy): 50 representative Q&As from past support tickets; hand-grade top-5 recall. Target ≥80%.\n  - Run V2 (cost baseline): 100 live queries; track total spend and p50/p95/p99. Target <$0.05/query.\n  - Run V3 (latency): 1000 retrieval-only queries; measure latency distribution. Target p99 <2s retrieval (excl. LLM).\n\n*Rollback:* Revert code to Phase 0; retrieval index can be deleted (re-indexed from Phase 0 mock data if needed); no breaking changes to API.\n\n*Exit checks:* V1 ≥80% recall, V2 <$0.05/query, V3 p99 <2s retrieval. All three gates pass before Phase 2 hardening begins.\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Prompt injection input/output filters active; cost counter enforced; rate limiting on LLM API calls (no unlimited retries).\n- **Observability:** Full request/response logging (question, retrieval results, LLM prompt/response, cost, latency); metrics dashboard created; alerts configured.\n- **Reproducibility:** Help-center doc snapshot versioned (commit hash in log); embedding model version tagged; retrieval index version labeled.\n- **Resilience:** Fallback to BM25 if vector DB times out; exponential backoff on LLM rate limits; graceful error message to user if retrievals fail (don't hang).\n\n---\n\n### Phase 2 (days 11–17): Safety Hardening + Human Review\n*Unlocks:* Human review loop validated and operational; compliance review passed; ready for closed-beta internal deployment.\n*Depends on:* Phase 1 exit checks passed (V1, V2, V3 all green); real support team assigned (1 reviewer minimum).\n*Tasks:*\n- **Human review queue:**\n  - Build simple UI: support team sees flagged answers (confidence <0.7), marks as \"correct,\" \"needs edit,\" or \"hallucination.\"\n  - Timestamp and log each review; track SLA (target: 95% reviewed within 30 min).\n  - Implement queue backoff: if reviews back up >5 min, chatbot temporarily stops accepting new questions and queues them.\n- **Compliance review:**\n  - Data classification audit: does help-center contain PII, regulated data (HIPAA, GDPR, PCI-DSS)?\n  - If yes: mask PII before retrieval context goes to LLM (e.g., redact customer email patterns from examples).\n  - Sign-off from legal/privacy: data residency, retention, audit trail, export/deletion procedures.\n- **CSAT & quality feedback:**\n  - Add simple 1–5 star rating to each chatbot answer.\n  - Log CSAT per session; correlate with confidence score, human review outcome.\n  - Collect correction feedback from support team (what did the chatbot get wrong?).\n- **A/B framework (optional):**\n  - For internal users only: 50% see chatbot, 50% see support queue. Track resolution rate, time-to-answer.\n- **Validation gates:**\n  - Run V4 (human review capacity): 1 reviewer processes 3 hours of peak simulated traffic (100 messages). Measure: queue depth, review latency. Target: 95% <30 min.\n  - Compliance review checklist: legal sign-off on PII handling, data residency, audit logging. Target: \"pass\" from privacy team.\n\n*Rollback:* Revert code to Phase 1; human review queue logs are kept for audit; no customer data exposed yet (internal team only).\n\n*Exit checks:* V4 (human review SLA met), compliance review passed, CSAT baseline captured (internal team), support team trained on queue.\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Compliance sign-off required; PII masking in retrieval context; human review logs stored separately (sensitive).\n- **Observability:** Human review queue dashboarded (queue depth, review latency, correction frequency); CSAT trend tracked; correction signals logged separately for future model tuning.\n- **Reproducibility:** Help-center snapshot locked per session (so corrections reference the exact docs); human review feedback versioned (commit per week).\n- **Resilience:** If review queue backs up >5 min, chatbot enters \"queue-only mode\" (accepts questions but routes to support fallback); manual override to close queue if needed.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 3 (days 18–24): Canary Exposure + Monitored Rollout\n*Unlocks:* Public chatbot for all customers; 100% traffic once canary SLO met.\n*Depends on:* Phase 2 exit checks passed (V4, compliance review, internal CSAT); runbook + on-call setup ready; support team trained on escalation.\n*Tasks:*\n- **Canary deployment (5% traffic):**\n  - Feature flag: route 5% of support traffic to chatbot, 95% to traditional support queue (baseline).\n  - Monitor for 1 week: latency, error rate, CSAT, escalation rate.\n  - Automatic circuit breaker: if p99 latency >5s OR error rate >1% OR CSAT <4.0/5 for >1 hour, rollback feature flag (revert to 100% traditional support).\n- **Runbook & on-call:**\n  - Escalation path: support team sees alert → checks dashboard → decides (override queue back open, or extend canary).\n  - Manual override: support lead can toggle feature flag and adjust traffic split without deploy.\n  - Rollback procedure: feature flag off; chatbot stops accepting new questions; in-flight questions time out gracefully.\n- **Real-time observability:**\n  - Support team dashboard: CSAT trend, top unanswered questions, error log, chatbot load vs. capacity.\n  - Alerts: CSAT drop, error spike, latency spike, cost per query spike, queue backup.\n- **Canary metrics validation:**\n  - Run V5 gate: after 1 week at 5%, check: p99 latency <5s, uptime >99.5%, CSAT >4.0/5, error rate <1%. Support lead sign-off required.\n  - If V5 passes: promote to 100% (feature flag opens to all traffic).\n  - If V5 fails: extend canary (1 more week) or rollback to Phase 2 (internal only).\n\n*Rollback:* Feature flag off → all traffic to traditional support; no data loss (all interactions logged in chatbot queue for post-mortem).\n\n*Point of no return:* Once chatbot handles >50% of live traffic and CSAT trend is negative for >1 hour, circuit breaker auto-triggers rollback (no manual approval needed).\n\n*Exit checks:* V5 gate passed; runbook tested once; support team alerts ACKed and responded to; 1 week of 5% canary stable.\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Rate limiting per user (max 10 questions/min); IP-based blocking for injection-like patterns; all interactions audit-logged (compliance required).\n- **Observability:** Real-time dashboards (support team), automated alerting (Slack/email to on-call), CSAT + error + latency tracked per hour, canary metrics published.\n- **Reproducibility:** Chatbot response version + LLM model version + help-center snapshot version logged per interaction (for debugging); corrections logged separately.\n- **Resilience:** Circuit breaker on error spike (auto-rollback); graceful \"contact support\" fallback if LLM unavailable; cost cutoff switch ($X/hour hard limit, stops new queries).\n\n---\n\n### Phase 4 (day 25+): Optimization & Feedback Loop\n*Unlocks:* Fine-tuning candidates identified; advanced routing (deferred); multi-language support (deferred); performance improvements.\n*Depends on:* Phase 3 canary + full launch stable for 2+ weeks; feedback data collected.\n*Tasks:*\n- **Analyze human corrections & CSAT:**\n  - Identify systematic hallucinations (e.g., \"reset password\" always says \"click Settings\" but some docs say \"Account Preferences\").\n  - Identify retrieval gaps (questions where top-5 docs don't contain answer).\n  - Identify high-value reductions (if 20 questions converge on same doc, consider adding FAQ link).\n- **Iterate on retrieval:**\n  - Re-chunk help docs if needed (smaller or larger chunks?).\n  - Adjust embedding model or ranking weights based on CSAT correlation.\n  - Add new docs or update stale docs based on correction feedback.\n- **Optional fine-tuning (data-driven):**\n  - If >100 high-confidence (CSAT=5, no hallucination) interactions are collected, consider fine-tuning LLM on these (Phase 4+).\n  - Test fine-tune on isolated cohort before production rollout.\n- **Prepare deferred work (not implemented yet):**\n  - Multi-language support: collect non-English queries; plan embedding + docs translation (Phase 5).\n  - Advanced routing: analyze if simple vs. complex questions diverge in accuracy; plan route model (Phase 5).\n  - CRM integration: support team requests; plan ticket linkage and data sync (Phase 5).\n\n*Rollback:* N/A (feedback loop; no breaking changes).\n\n*Exit checks:* Correction feedback analyzed; top 5 hallucination patterns identified and logged; retrieval re-ranking evaluated (before/after CSAT impact).\n\n*Cross-cutting moves:*\n- **Security:** Audit corrections for PII leaks; redact future retrieval contexts if patterns found; tighten output filters.\n- **Observability:** Feedback loop dashboard (correction types, hallucination frequency, retrieval miss rate); model performance drift detection (CSAT trend).\n- **Reproducibility:** Fine-tuning datasets versioned (if used); model checkpoint + version tagged per week; before/after metrics compared.\n- **Resilience:** Monitor for feedback loop collapse (corrections amplifying errors); keep human review threshold high until retraining validates.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Validation Gates\n\n| Gate ID | Hypothesis | Method | Acceptance threshold | Evidence | Unlocks | Phase |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **V0** | Real request reaches LLM end-to-end and returns answer; pipeline deploys and rolls back cleanly | One internal user asks \"How do I reset my password?\"; answer logged with trace ID; deploy to prod via CI/CD; rollback to prior image | Answer returned within 30s with trace visible; deploy <2 min; rollback <1 min; error injection triggers alert; baseline latency (cold start, warm start) recorded | CloudWatch JSON log with full request/response/trace ID; ECR image tags; CI/CD run history showing deploy + rollback | Phase 1 retrieval + Phase 1 guardrails work begins | Phase 0 (days 1–3) |\n| **V1** | Retrieval returns relevant docs in top-5 with ≥80% recall | 50 representative Q&As from past support tickets; run each through live retrieval; hand-grade whether correct answer appears in top-5 | ≥80% of questions have relevant doc in top-5; median rank of correct answer ≤2 | CSV: question_id, top_5_doc_ids, human_graded_recall (Y/N), rank_of_correct_doc | Phase 1 hardening complete; V1 failure triggers retrieval re-plan (expand index, adjust ranking, or pivot strategy) | Phase 1 (starts day 4, required before Phase 2) |\n| **V2** | Cost per query is economically viable | 100 representative queries run through live LLM API + embedding endpoint; record token counts and API charges | <$0.05/query median; <$0.10/query p95 (includes embedding + LLM call) | JSON metrics: query_id, embedding_cost, llm_input_tokens, llm_output_tokens, llm_cost, total_cost; API provider receipts | Phase 1 hardening; cost model validated; if >$0.10 p95, consider cheaper model or retrieval optimization | Phase 1 (starts day 4, required before Phase 2) |\n| **V3** | Retrieval latency meets SLO (p99 <2s) | 1000 retrieval-only queries (embeddings + vector DB + ranking, excl. LLM); measure latency | p50 <500ms; p95 <1s; p99 <2s | Latency histogram from benchmarking harness; time-series plot of p50/p95/p99 | Phase 1 hardening; if failed, evaluate BM25 fallback or index tuning; signals readiness for LLM integration | Phase 1 (starts day 4, required before Phase 2) |\n| **V4** | Human review team can sustain 95% of reviews within 30-min SLO | Simulate 3 hours of peak traffic (100 msgs/hour); 1 reviewer processes queue; measure time-to-review and queue depth | 95% of answers reviewed within 30 min; reviewer does not exceed 3 msgs/min (sustainable pace); queue never exceeds 10 items | Timestamped review queue log; time-to-review histogram; reviewer workload estimate | Phase 2 hardening; determines if human-in-the-loop gating is operationally viable; V4 failure blocks Phase 3 or triggers queue automation (auto-escalate high-confidence answers) | Phase 2 (starts day 11, required before Phase 3) |\n| **V5** | System meets SLO and customer experience bar in 5% canary | Route 5% of live traffic to chatbot for 1 week; measure latency, uptime, CSAT, error rate | p99 latency <5s; uptime >99.5%; CSAT >4.0/5 (from 1–5 rating); error rate <1% | Canary metrics dashboard exported as CSV; CSAT survey results; error logs from CloudWatch | Phase 3 → full 100% launch; auto-rollback if V5 metric breaches for >1 hour | Phase 3 (starts day 18, required before 100% traffic) |\n| **Compliance Review** | Help-center data can be legally exposed to LLM inference without violating privacy/security obligations | Legal + Privacy team audits help-center docs for PII, regulated data (HIPAA, GDPR, PCI-DSS); reviews LLM vendor data handling; approves retrieval context masking rules | Sign-off from Privacy Lead on: (1) PII masking rules (email/phone redacted), (2) data residency acceptable, (3) audit logging sufficient | Email approval from Privacy team; documented PII masking rules; audit log format | Phase 2 exit; Phase 3 canary launch; V0 if fails (no exposure until resolved) | Phase 2 (starts day 11, required before Phase 3) |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Cross-cutting Concerns\n\n| Concern | Phase 0 | Phase 1 | Phase 2 | Phase 3 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **Security** | Hard token limit (500 out); session cost cap ($1); mock docs only (no real data) | Prompt injection input filter (jailbreak detection); output filter (PII redaction, contradiction flagging); token counter enforced; rate limiting on LLM API | Compliance clearance gate (Privacy team sign-off on PII handling); role-based access control for human review queue (support team only); audit log separate from application logs | Rate limiting per user (10 q/min); IP-based blocking for injection patterns; real-time audit logging; circuit breaker on injection spike; all interactions audit-logged with user context |\n| **Observability** | Structured JSON logging to stdout (trace ID, latency, cost); latency + cost metrics exported to CSV at phase end; no dashboard yet | Query-level logging (question, top-5 doc IDs, LLM prompt first 200 chars, tokens, cost, latency, user anonymized); metrics dashboard created (latency histogram, cost distribution, retrieval recall); alerts on cost spike (>2x baseline) | Human review queue metrics dashboarded (queue depth, review latency p50/p95, correction frequency); CSAT trend tracked per day; correction signals logged separately | Real-time dashboards for support team (CSAT trend, top unanswered questions, error log, chatbot QPS vs. capacity); automated alerts to Slack/email on SLO breach (latency >5s, CSAT <4.0, error >1%); canary metrics published hourly |\n| **Reproducibility** | Code in Git (main branch); requirements.txt pinned (all deps versioned); Docker image tagged with Git SHA; mock data set versioned | Help-center doc snapshot tagged (commit hash logged); embedding model version logged; retrieval index version labeled; V1/V2/V3 benchmark data saved | Same as Phase 1; human review feedback versioned (commit per week); PII masking rule version tracked | Same as Phase 2; model version + LLM provider + help-center version logged per interaction (for debugging); canary traffic split + feature flag version logged |\n| **Resilience** | N/A (internal only; graceful failure acceptable; no SLO) | Fallback to BM25 if vector DB times out; exponential backoff on LLM rate limits; timeout guard (5s) on all external calls; graceful error response (\"Contact support\") if retrieval fails | If human review queue backs up >5 min, chatbot enters read-only mode (queues questions, routes to support fallback); manual override available to support lead to close queue | Circuit breaker: if p99 latency >5s OR error >1% OR CSAT <4.0 for >1 hour → auto-rollback feature flag (stop 100% launch, revert to canary or Phase 2); cost cutoff switch ($X/hour hard limit stops new queries); graceful \"Contact support\" fallback if LLM unavailable |\n\n---\n\n## 8. AI Layer\n\nPer principle 7: **defenses → budgets → retrieval → model access → memory → orchestration → routing → feedback**.\n\n| Sublayer | What it is here | Phase built | Exit check / V-ID |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **Guardrails / Prompt injection defense** | Input filter: regex patterns for jailbreak (SQL, code execution, role-switching attempts); output filter: redact email/phone; flag contradictions vs. top docs | Phase 1 | V1 recall check validates no malicious prompts escape to LLM; output filter tested on 20 injection payloads (must redact/flag all) |\n| **Cost + latency budget** | Hard token cap: max 500 output tokens per response (auto-truncate); per-session cap: max $1 (stop all queries if exceeded); retrieval + LLM latency SLO: p99 <5s | Phase 0 (skeleton hard limits); Phase 1 (soft tracking) | V2 gate: <$0.05/query median validates cost model sustainable; V3 gate: p99 <2s retrieval latency validates feasibility |\n| **Human-in-the-loop gating** | Flag answers with confidence <0.7 for support-team review queue before customer sees them; CSAT + correction feedback loop | Phase 2 | V4 gate: human review SLA (95% <30 min) validates operationally feasible; Phase 3 canary: rollback if >5% of answers require correction |\n| **Retrieval** | Hybrid search: BM25 (lexical) + dense vector (semantic); chunk docs 512 tokens, 50% overlap; re-rank by recency + doc quality score + relevance confidence | Phase 1 | V1 gate: 80%+ recall in top-5 validates retrieval strategy; if <70%, replan to pure BM25 or expand index |\n| **Model access** | Hosted LLM API (assume Anthropic Claude for this plan; fallback to OpenAI if contract not available); embeddings from same vendor or Cohere | Phase 0 (mock); Phase 1 (live integration) | LLM provider contract signed (organizational check); rate limits confirmed; fallback provider identified by Phase 1; live API test in V0 |\n| **Memory** | Session-scoped conversation history (no persistent user profile); question-answer pairs only (not full chat); ephemeral by design | Phase 2 (scope confirmed) | Deferred: multi-turn context; re-evaluate in Phase 3 if CSAT feedback warrants (users want history) |\n| **Orchestration** | Simple linear pipeline: validate input → embed query → retrieve docs → construct prompt → call LLM → format output → log → return | Phase 0 | V0 gate: one trace through all steps succeeds |\n| **Routing** | Single LLM model for all question types (no routing logic); deferred: separate models for simple vs. complex questions | Phase 0–2 | Single-model accuracy baseline established in Phase 2 A/B test; re-evaluate in Phase 4 if accuracy diverges by question type |\n| **Feedback** | Structured logging of: human review corrections, CSAT ratings, retrieval quality (correct doc in top-5?), hallucinations flagged, cost/latency skew | Phase 2 | V4 gate: >100 labeled correction examples collected per week by Phase 2 validates feedback loop density; Phase 4 analysis determines if fine-tuning warranted |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Methodology Exceptions\n\n**None.** Standard dependency-first order applies. Walking skeleton (V0), defenses (guardrails, budgets), retrieval validation (V1–V3), human-in-the-loop (V4), compliance review, canary SLO (V5), and feedback loop proceed in sequence. No time-boxed spike or exceptional constraints justify reordering.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Deliberately Deferred\n\n| Feature | Condition to pull forward | Why deferred now |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Multi-language support** | If >20% of support traffic is non-English (measured in Phase 3) | English-only in Phase 0–2 simplifies Phase 0 skeleton; i18n of help docs + embeddings is R2 refactor that can wait until demand signals it |\n| **Advanced routing (question-type-specific models)** | If accuracy diverges >5% between \"technical\" vs. \"billing\" questions (measured in Phase 2 feedback) | Single model is simpler for Phase 0; routing adds orchestration complexity; wait for data to justify it |\n| **CRM / ticketing integration** | If support team requests it post-Phase 3 (e.g., \"link chat answers to tickets\") | Read-only help-center wrapper in Phase 0–3 is sufficient to validate core RAG quality; integration is organizational/process work, not critical path to launch |\n| **Persistent chat history & multi-turn context** | If Phase 3 CSAT analysis shows users want context retention (e.g., \"remember my previous question\") | Session-scoped conversation is simpler for Phase 0–2; adding history requires user model, retention policies (GDPR?), and persistence layer; wait for feedback |\n| **Fine-tuning on corrected interactions** | If Phase 3 feedback loop yields >500 high-confidence corrected interactions AND systematic hallucination pattern emerges | Fine-tuning is R3 (model retraining, reproducibility, version control); Phase 4 is earliest if data density justifies it; meanwhile, retrieval re-ranking and doc updates are faster wins |\n| **Self-hosted LLM model** | If Phase 1 V2 gate shows cost >$0.10/query OR compliance review mandates on-prem data residency | Hosted API (Anthropic/OpenAI) is faster to market; self-hosting adds ops burden (GPU provisioning, monitoring, failover); defer unless forced by economics or compliance |\n\n---\n\n## Self-Check\n\n✅ **Phase 0 is a production walking skeleton:** REST API endpoint, real question routed through every tier (embedding, retrieval, LLM), response logged with trace ID, deployed via CI/CD pipeline, rollback tested. Internal-only (feature flag or network isolation). Runs with unvalidated defaults (no guardrails, no human review, mock docs).\n\n✅ **Tasks follow dependency order:** V0 skeleton → Phase 1 (retrieval quality V1 + cost V2 + latency V3 + guardrails) → Phase 2 (human review SLA V4 + compliance review) → Phase 3 (canary SLO V5 + runbook) → Phase 4 (feedback loop + optimization).\n\n✅ **Rework runs widest first, exposure smallest first:** Retrieval architecture (V1 recall) and cost model (V2) validated before human review queue added (Phase 1 before Phase 2). Exposure progresses: internal only → 5% canary (1 week) → 100% launch.\n\n✅ **Each R3 tradeoff has a default, assumption, flip condition, and V-gate:** RAG vs. fine-tune (default RAG, flip if V1 <70%), hosted API vs. self-hosted (default hosted, flip if V2 >$0.10 or compliance fails).\n\n✅ **Each gate has five fields, assigned phase, and a clear consumer:** V0 unlocks Phase 1, V1 unlocks retrieval hardening, V2 unlocks cost model lock, V3 unlocks LLM integration, V4 unlocks Phase 2 exit, V5 unlocks 100% launch.\n\n✅ **Thresholds labeled:** ASSUMPTION (<$0.05/query, <5s latency, 80% recall), REQUIREMENT (99.5% SLO inferred from customer support), UNKNOWN (help-center volume, compliance rules, LLM provider—all noted as missing inputs in Step 1).\n\n✅ **Cross-cutting moves in every phase:** Security (hard limits → filters → compliance → audit), observability (JSON logs → metrics dashboard → real-time alerts), reproducibility (Git + versions → snapshots → tagged models), resilience (timeouts → fallback search → queue backoff → circuit breaker).\n\n✅ **AI layer:** Defenses (guardrails V1 gate) → budgets (V2/V3 gates) → retrieval (V1 gate) → model access (LLM provider) → memory (session-scoped, deferred) → orchestration (linear pipeline V0) → routing (single model, deferred) → feedback (logged in Phase 2).\n\n✅ **No silent exceptions:** Standard order applies; all deferred work has pull-forward conditions.\n\n✅ **Nothing the request doesn't need:** No gates without consumers, no redundant content, no gates that would not change the plan if they fail.\n\n---\n\n**This plan is ready for implementation.** Start with **V0 (Phase 0):** build the skeleton, validate end-to-end, record baselines.
